Wait, let me look at this differently. The &quot;HxD&quot; motif (H242-T-D244) is a classic non-heme iron binding motif. The second His might be elsewhere.

In many non-heme iron oxygenases:
- The catalytic Fe is bound by 2 His and 1 Asp/Glu (facial triad)
- The HxD...H motif is common

If H242 and D244 form the &quot;HxD&quot; part, then another His would complete the triad. H298 could serve as that His.

Arg308 (R308) is an arginine - arginines typically don&#x27;t directly coordinate iron ions. They might be involved in substrate binding or protein-protein interactions.

So for Fe-cation binding specifically (the catalytic non-heme iron), the binding residues would be:
- H242 (His)
- D244 (Asp)  
- H298 (His)

These form the classic facial triad (His-Asp-His) for non-heme iron binding.

Arg308 is not a metal-binding residue - arginine&#x27;s side chain (guanidinium group) doesn&#x27;t coordinate metal ions effectively.

So the answer should be A: His242, Asp244, His298.

Let me double-check: The question asks about &quot;Fe-cation binding rather than with the protein&#x27;s other ligand.&quot; This suggests there are multiple ligands/binding sites, and we need to identify which residues specifically bind Fe.

For a non-heme iron site, the facial triad of 2His+1Asp is the canonical binding motif. This matches option A: His242, Asp244, His298.

Arg308 would not be involved in direct Fe coordination, as arginine is not a typical metal-coordinating residue.

The answer is A.
